Signedness rides the integer type as a flag rather than as a new type kind, so every lowering that never asks about sign stays correct by construction, add, subtract, multiply, the bitwise trio, `<<`, and equality among them, and the sign sensitive list stays short and auditable: divide, remainder, right shift, the four ordered comparisons, widening, both float conversions, printing, and `hash`. The surface rule is one word, the same word width already carried: signedness never mixes. This is a widening only change, four type names and four suffixes no legal program could spell before, and the compiler, the standard library, and the tools in this tree spell none of them, so the previous release builds this source unchanged; the adoption sweep comes later.

The types.

- `uint8`, `uint16`, `uint32`, and `uint64` are types an annotation names, a struct field, an array element, a slice element, a tuple member, a parameter, and a return type carry, and the suffixes `u8`, `u16`, `u32`, and `u64` pin a literal to one without an annotation. Arithmetic wraps at the width exactly as signed arithmetic already wraps, so an unsigned type adds no new trap and no new fault.
- A `u` suffixed literal names a bit pattern rather than a signed magnitude, so the lexer ranges it against the unsigned top instead of the `int64` one and `18446744073709551615u64` is written directly and prints `18446744073709551615`. An unsuffixed literal keeps the `int64` ceiling, and above `2^64 - 1` a literal is `invalid integer literal` whatever the suffix. A suffixed literal too wide for the type it lands in meets the ordinary fit rule and names the value it read, `literal 18446744073709551615 does not fit in uint32`.
- An unsigned type is a distinct type all the way down, not a signed one wearing a flag. It interns distinctly from the signed twin of the same width, so `uint32` and `int32` are never the same type identity even though both lower to an LLVM `i32`, and a generic instantiated at an unsigned width mangles to its own instantiation with its own emitted body. The distinctness survives every round trip the pipeline makes through a type name, so a checked unsigned type cannot come back out of monomorphization as its signed twin.
- Everything sign sensitive follows the operand's own type. Division and remainder take `udiv` and `urem`, `>>` takes `lshr` so a zero comes down into the top bit rather than a copy of the sign, the four ordered comparisons take their `u` forms, widening takes `zext`, an unsigned value crosses to a float with `uitofp`, a float crosses to an unsigned width with a saturating `fptoui`, printing writes unsigned decimal, and `hash` widens by zero extension. Extension follows the source type, never the destination, which is what keeps a `uint8` holding `255` from arriving at an `int64` as `-1`.

The rules.

- Signedness never mixes, across arithmetic, comparison, the bitwise operators, the shifts, `**`, assignment, and argument passing. The diagnostic names which of the two rules broke: a width disagreement still ends `; match the widths`, and a signedness disagreement at one width ends `; cast to one signedness`, so a program reads `arithmetic mixes int32 and uint32; cast to one signedness`, `comparison mixes uint32 and int32; cast to one signedness`, `'&' mixes uint32 and int32; cast to one signedness`, and `'**' mixes uint32 and int32; cast to one signedness`. A cross signedness annotated bind and a cross signedness argument reject through the existing annotation and argument messages.
- A bare literal adapts to the signedness beside it the way it already adapts to the width, so `5u32 + 1` is a `uint32` and the unsuffixed operand never drags the result back to the signed twin codegen would then divide, shift, and compare the wrong way.
- A literal in an unsigned position is checked against `[0, 2^w - 1]`, so `x: uint8 = 256` is `literal 256 does not fit in uint8`, naming the type rather than a bit count, since the bit count alone would read as though the bit pattern were the question. A negative literal has no unsigned value at any width and is refused outright, `literal -1 does not fit in uint32; an unsigned type holds no negative value, cast if the wrap is meant`, and the reject follows the type into every position a literal adapts into: an annotated binding, an assignment, a compound assignment, a call argument, a return, a struct literal field, an enum constructor payload, an array or slice literal element at any nesting depth, a tuple literal member against an annotation or against a return, a destructured binder, the tail of a `match` arm, a bare operand beside an unsigned value so `-1 + u` is refused, and a `collector<T>` mint.
- Unary minus takes a signed integer or a float. Negating an unsigned value wraps to the top of its range instead of going below zero, almost always a width bug, so it is refused, `cannot negate uint32; an unsigned value has no negation, cast to a signed width first`.
- An index and a range endpoint are `int64` offsets, so an unsigned one is refused on the surface where the cast is a one word fix, `cannot index with uint32; an index is int64, cast the index`, rather than left for the widening to pick an extension by a signedness the index position never states.
- The four names carry the primitive type name reservation the signed widths already carried, so a top level function cannot be declared `uint32`, `'uint32' is a primitive type name; a function cannot take it`. A variable or a field of that name is still legal.
- `**` on an unsigned pair is the same repeated squaring the signed form runs and wraps at the operands' own width, so `2u64 ** 3u64` is `8` and `65536u32 ** 2u32` is `0`, the exact power wrapped away at 32 bits. An unsigned exponent is never negative, so the negative exponent fault cannot arise on an unsigned pair however large the exponent runs, and `1u64 ** e` for an `e` at the top of `uint64` is `1` rather than a trap.
- A generic body cannot be width checked while its parameter is still a hole, so a mixed signedness instantiation is caught by the ground pass that re-runs the type class after monomorphization makes every type concrete, reporting the same mixing diagnostic a concrete body gets.

The conversions.

- `uint8(v)`, `uint16(v)`, `uint32(v)`, and `uint64(v)` join the cast builtins, bringing the family to twelve names. A cast is unchecked, as it always has been. Same width across signedness is a bit reinterpretation that moves nothing, `uint8(-1)` giving `255` and `int8(255u8)` giving `-1`. Narrowing truncates, `uint8(300)` giving `44`. Widening extends by the source's signedness, `uint64(int8(-1))` giving `18446744073709551615` and `uint64(uint8(-1))` giving `255`.
- A float to an unsigned width saturates into `[0, 2^w - 1]` rather than wrapping or leaving the result undefined, so `uint8(-5.0)` is `0`, `uint16(70000.0)` is `65535`, and a value past every width's top lands on that width's top; a NaN casts to `0`. An unsigned integer to a float goes by magnitude, exactly when the value fits the significand and rounded past it.
- An unsigned value crosses the foreign boundary as the same LLVM `i<w>` its signed twin crosses as, so a `foreign` declaration takes one as a parameter or a return. The C header a `dusk build --lib` writes spells it `uint<w>_t`, since the header is what a C caller compiles against and an `int<w>_t` there would make the C side read a large unsigned value as negative.
- `print`, `println`, `printerr`, a `{}` format hole, and an f-string hole all render an unsigned value as unsigned decimal through one shared sink, so a `uint64` at the top of its range prints `18446744073709551615` and `println("{}", v)` and `f"{v}"` write byte identical output. A signed only program's emitted declare block is unchanged, since the unsigned printers are declared on demand.
- `hash` accepts an unsigned operand and widens it by zero extension, so a `Map<uint64, V>` keys across the whole width. `hash(4294967295u32)` and the hash of an `int32` holding the same bits differ, which is the coherent answer rather than a collision: the two values are not equal and do not even compare across signedness, so a hash that agreed on them would break the guarantee a map leans on.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 1012 records, 38 new since 1.12.0: the type carrying run across a struct field, an array, a slice, a tuple, and a function boundary, the literal range and result type check, the sign sensitive operator run pairing every unsigned form against its signed twin over the same bits, the cast matrix covering reinterpretation, truncation, both extensions, float saturation at both bounds, NaN, and the `uint64` top, and the reject twins for arithmetic, comparison, bitwise, and `**` mixing, the negative literal at every checked position, a literal past the unsigned top, unary minus, an unsigned index and range endpoint, a cross signedness bind, argument, and result, a function named `uint32`, and the ground pass instantiation.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.12.0` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (`f8903da560b40a556558e53acea51fc676c29a984aedf0791fd84395ddfe67b2`) and one compiler IR sha256 (`78d2a1cd318ac2d479e624077c9dd5520122e55bf0e38d3af995e9dbaad7299d`), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 1012 of 1012 under both of the ladder's own suite runs. The ratchet holds: the `v1.12.0` release binary builds the 1.13.0 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green. The IR harness holds: a program that names no unsigned type lowers byte identically, since the sign flag never changes the LLVM type an integer spells.

## 1.12.0

The hunt and the reference. dusk spends a release consolidating before the adoption sweep: a systematic read of the whole existing surface for the silent bug classes recent lines kept finding only where a diff happened to graze one, the carried debt ledger dispositioned item by item, and a standard library documentation pass that turns `dusk doc` into a real reference. Three sweeps drove it: an audit of every checker site that gates an obligation on a provable type, an audit of every code generation default that types a slot or picks a width when resolution fails alongside a per-file enumeration of every function that recurses an expression, and a scan for shapes that pass `dusk check` and then die in `clang`. Every fix is raise-only or the repair of a probed wrong behavior, so the previous release builds this source unchanged; the surface tightens where it was silently loose and widens in one place, an `async func` taking a `using` allocator.

The hunt.

- Generic bodies gain the ownership discipline they never had. Inside a generic body a value whose type is a bare type parameter borrows, so `free` and `move` on one are refused with the same messages a concrete borrow gives, `cannot free a borrowed pointer; only its owner frees it` and `cannot move a borrowed pointer; only its owner can be moved`, once monomorphization would instantiate the parameter with a managed pointer the caller owns. The rule reaches every position a borrowed name enters or travels: a `func f<T>(p: T)` parameter, a lambda parameter typed `T`, a plain binding or a rebind, a member destructured out of a tuple a type parameter value was laundered through, the capture of a task lambda spawned over one, and the left side of a reassignment that would drop a value the binder owns at instantiation. The concrete twin already rejected the same `free`; the generic form now matches rather than admitting a double free the ground instantiation would only find at runtime. The tightening is one-sided by design and said plainly: a bare `T` binder is a borrow for the restrictive obligations but is never promoted to an owner, since marking it an owner would false-reject a legal scalar copy at a non pointer instantiation, `dup(5)` and `n: int64 = take_id(41)`, where the value is an `int64` that copies freely and owns nothing. So a generic body still copies and returns a type parameter value as a borrow the caller keeps, and an `owning func take_id<T>(x: T) -> T` is an owner at a managed instantiation and inert at a scalar one. The durable close of the whole family is a ground ownership pass judging each obligation on the substituted type after mono; until it lands the borrow-side pessimism ships and the owner-side stays conservative, and every residual slip the generational backstop turns into a named fault rather than a silent corruption.
- A match expression stops truncating its result silently. The single result slot a match expression's arms share sizes from the arm types, and several arm shapes fell to the word-width default when the slot typing could not read them, so a match whose arms are float literals printed `2` for `2.5`, and arms that are a cast, a closure call, an interface method call, a negation, or a binary compound truncated the same way, each check-clean and wrong at runtime. The slot typing now carries a targeted arm for every one of these, so the width comes from the arm's own type rather than a word-sized fallback. The general fallback for a genuinely unresolvable arm stays ledgered rather than made loud, since a blanket reject would false-reject a working word-width program; the targeted arms close the confirmed silent classes.
- Tuples with tuple, closure, or interface members build instead of dying in `clang`. The type-to-AST encoding lacked arms for those three member shapes, so a legitimate tuple carrying a nested tuple, a closure value, or a boxed interface split five ways at build after checking clean. The missing arms are added, so a nested tuple, a tuple with a closure element, a tuple with an interface element, an interface tuple passed as an argument or returned, a struct field of interface type, and an enum variant carrying an interface payload each build and run.
- An interface value returned inside a carrier is sealed. Adding the tuple member encoding above turned a loud `clang` death into a silent read of a dead frame: an interface value returned inside a tuple could now be built, and its data pointer still named the returning frame's boxed concrete value, so the caller read a reclaimed slot. The return walk now refuses an interface value embedded at any depth in the declared return type, a tuple member, a struct or embedded field, an enum payload, an array element, and the pointee of a returned pointer, each with `returning an interface value is not supported; return the concrete type or a pointer to it`, so `-> (Shape, int64)` and `-> *Shape` from a frame local are caught at check exactly as a bare `-> Shape` is.
- A builtin call name is reserved at every binding site. A function declared with the name of a builtin the checker and code generator resolve first, `move`, `alloc`, `free`, `sizeof`, `hash`, the print family, or a functional builtin, is refused, `'<name>' is a builtin; a function cannot take its name`, and the same name is refused as a parameter, a `using` allocator, or a local binding, `'<name>' is a builtin; a binding cannot take its name`, the parameter reject pointing at the function head. This closes a confirmed class where a user `move` was silently dead code the builtin shadowed, a user `println` swallowed its own output, and an owning `alloc` split at `clang`; it is one coherent reservation across the whole builtin call table, raise-only over declarations that never had a working meaning.
- A brace written as a Unicode escape in a format string is a literal brace. `\u{7b}` and `\u{7d}` each decode to one literal brace the format scan writes straight through, distinct from a placeholder brace typed directly, so `println("A\u{7b}B")` prints `A{B`, `println("\u{7b}\u{7b}")` prints two braces with no fold, and a real placeholder still expands between escaped braces, `println("val=\u{7b}{}\u{7d}", 5)` printing `val={5}`. The f-string raw-span cook that already reads a chunk's escapes before its braces is ported to the format path, so before this release `\u{7b}\u{7b}` folded to one brace and `A\u{7b}B` was wrongly rejected as an unmatched placeholder; both are now correct.
- An `async func` takes a `using` allocator. The parameter lands in the heap task frame like any other, and bare `alloc` inside the body resolves the ambient allocator by name through the frame, so the passed arena backs the allocation rather than the default heap. The allocator survives a suspension: an allocation before an `await` and one after both draw from the same arena, since the poll re-enters at the await label and re-reads the allocator from its frame slot rather than losing it across the resume edge. This retires the loud reject the feature carried, `an async func cannot take a using parameter yet`, and is verified across a plain body, a body with a suspension, and a suspension inside a loop, each with IR-checked per-site frame reloads.
- The remaining ledger and diagnostics items close. A lambda literal called where it is written, the immediately invoked form `lambda (...) { ... }(x)` and its parenthesized twin, is refused with a teaching message, `a lambda literal cannot be called where it is written; bind it to a name and call the name`, since the checker lowers a lambda through the name it is bound to. `sizeof` of an inline tuple type is refused, `sizeof takes a type or a value; a tuple type is written through a named struct`, consulting local scopes first so a tuple valued binding still sizes as a value. A `defer` on an illegal registration site points its caret at the keyword span rather than the whole statement. And a dead scanner with zero call sites, re-confirmed unreachable, is deleted rather than carried.

The reference.

- Every export in the standard library carries a full doc comment in the 1.6.1 form, a summary sentence, `@param` prose for every parameter, `@return` prose for every non-void return, and `@example` where a shape is not obvious, across all 42 standard library files, so `dusk doc` emits a reference a person would actually read. The compiler's highest-traffic top level items gain summaries so `dusk doc` over `compiler/` is navigable. The pass changes no signature and no behavior: `dusk check` verdicts are identical and IR moves only where a stdlib comment insertion shifts a fault-location line, which the pyramid re-fixes, so the only intended delta is the documentation output. The doc drift checker enforces `@param` names, so the sweep is self-verifying and `dusk doc` runs clean over every touched file.

The notes.

- Three items stay ledgered, named rather than hidden. The general result-slot fallback for a match arm no static case resolves stays a silent word-width default rather than a loud reject, since a blanket reject would false-reject a working word-width program; the targeted arms close every confirmed class and the fallback waits for a durable slot-typing pass. An `async func` with more than one `using` parameter takes the last, a last-wins wart left rather than rejected. And the interprocedural passthrough launder parity items, a borrow carried out through an aggregate a call returns, stay open on the summary side, tracked toward the field-read inference work a later release names.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 974 records, 45 new since 1.11.1: the generic-body borrow twins across param, lambda param, rebind, destructure, task capture, and reassign spellings, the match slot repairs for float, cast, closure, and interface arms, the nested, closure, and interface tuple runs, the interface return rejects at every depth, the builtin reservation twins with the scalar-name legal twin, the escaped-brace format run, the async using runs including the across-await arena, the IIFE and sizeof rejects, the defer caret pin, and the sizeof shadow and suffix repairs.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.11.1` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (9c0518785c3b1d7fb92f757f382c30859b54e374faaf6a853ed32cefd55ed3b2) and one compiler IR sha256 (ab0573cfd8d1f26480a98b75bd9da2c6a87ce215236db51a6694cf527b2a9534), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 974/974 under the ladder's own suite runs. The comment differential holds at 920 byte-equal comparisons over 972 files with zero failures. The ratchet holds: the `v1.11.1` release binary builds the 1.12.0 source, the reference's doc comments included, and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite and rebuilds its own test runner. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.

## 1.11.1

The interpolated string. dusk gains opt-in string interpolation: `f"total: {n}"` is an expression of type `string` whose holes render inline, byte-identical to what `println("{}", v)` writes for each value. A small lexer mode splits the literal into chunk and hole tokens, the type pass checks each hole against the printable set a `println` argument already uses, and a new runtime builder lowers the whole thing natively, so an f-string is an ordinary heap string that frees and concatenates like any other. This is a widening only surface change behind a prefix, an `f` before a `"`, no legal program spelled before, so the previous release builds this source unchanged; the tree adopts f-strings from 1.12.x on.

The interpolated string.

- `f"..."` is a `string` valued expression, an `f` on the opening quote following the `r'...'` rune precedent. A chunk between holes reads like a plain literal, the same escapes and `\u{...}` decode, and `{{` and `}}` cook to single braces; a hole `{expr}` holds an arbitrary expression, and holes may sit adjacent with empty chunks between them. A holeless `f"abc"` is a single-chunk f-string, not a plain literal: its `{{` and `}}` cook through the builder and it stays a value, so `println(f"...")` never rescans it as a format string. Each hole runs the existing `check_printable`, so its printable set is exactly a `println` argument's, an integer, a float, a `bool`, a `char`, a `char[N]`, a `char[]`, a `rune`, a `string`, an `error`, and a `Display` struct, and the rendered bytes are byte-identical to `println("{}", v)` minus the newline for every one of them, a struct through its `toString`, pinned by a parity golden.
- An f-string is not a format string. It carries no `{}` placeholder and no argument list; every brace pair doubles to a literal brace or holds an expression. As a `println` argument, `println(f"x{n}")`, it is one already-rendered string on the value path, never expanded, so `println` does not scan it for holes.
- A new lexer mode drives it. A byte `f` immediately before `"` dispatches the chunk scanner; a single `{` opens a hole and the lexer returns to ordinary token lexing under a brace-depth counter until the matching `}` closes it, so a struct literal, a lambda, a nested plain string, and a comment inside a hole balance by token-level counting rather than by rescanning text. The four token kinds `k_fstr_full`, `_head`, `_mid`, and `_tail` carry the pieces, and the parser builds an `xk_fstr` node whose kids strictly alternate chunk and hole, `2n+1` kids with the odd positions the holes, so every generic AST walker recurses them uniformly and only the code generator reads positions.
- Code generation lowers a hole through a new runtime builder, `cool_fsb_new`, `_cstr`, `_bytes`, `_i64`, `_f64`, and `_fin` in `runtime/runtime.c`, whose value dispatch mirrors the print path arm for arm, `PRId64` and `%g` shared with the existing print formats so parity holds by construction. The builder is a doubling scratch buffer; `_fin` copies the result into a generational-heap allocation, frees the scratch, and returns the string, so an f-string is the `cool_str_concat` allocation class, frees with an ordinary `free`, and feeds the string `+` operator. Inside an async body the builder and its intermediates live within one poll invocation, no `await` between them, so the whole render is straight-line.
- Five spellings are rejected, each named. An empty hole `f"a {} b"` is `an interpolation hole needs an expression; '{}' is the format placeholder for println, an f-string writes the expression inline`, distinguishing it from a format string's `{}`. A nested f-string `f"a {f"b"} c"` is `an interpolated string cannot nest inside another; bind the inner one first`. A bare `}` in a chunk is `unmatched '}' in an interpolated string; write '}}' for a literal brace`, stricter than the format language by design. An unclosed f-string is `unterminated string literal`. And an `await` inside a hole is refused as every mid-expression `await` is, `'await' cannot appear mid-expression; give the awaited value a name, as in v, e := await f`. A hole holding an unprintable value reuses the print diagnostics: an enum is `cannot print the enum '<name>'; match on it and print its parts`, a pointer is `cannot print a pointer; dereference it or print its fields`, and a void call is `cannot print a void value`.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 929 records, 22 new since 1.11.0: the parity golden asserting every printable type's hole bytes equal its println twin in-program, the escape, expression, lambda, async, concat, Display, and value-argument run goldens, the holeless value, escape-brace, match-arm, imported-qualified-call, and imported-span records, and the eight reject twins covering the enum, pointer, and void holes, the await hole, the empty hole, nesting, the stray chunk brace, and the unterminated form.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.11.0` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (fd908fc347ad77ed23b3cfadc168235baddfff6f6ad08b4f673f82d1c0c9fec7) and one compiler IR sha256 (1a0f0df9080288c857ebe134b2fdc5080145b296e8fee6f86a06364db5afc98d), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 929/929 under the ladder's own suite runs. The comment differential holds at 896 byte-equal comparisons over 926 files with zero failures. The ratchet holds: the `v1.11.0` release binary builds the 1.11.1 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green. The IR harness holds: every existing program lowers byte-identically, since a program with no interpolated string never reaches the new lowering.

## 1.11.0

The container loop. dusk lets `for` iterate the two standard containers: `for x in v` over a `*Vector<T>`, and `for k in m` and `for k, v in m` over a `*Map<K, V>`. The checker classifies the iterand on its type and the loop lowers, in a new pass between the surface type pass and monomorphization, into the exact index loop a program would write by hand against `vec_len` and `vec_get`, or `map_len` and two new positional map accessors, so container layout stays in the standard library and code generation never learns it. The rewrite is safer than a hand written snapshot: the length re-reads every iteration, so a container freed inside the body faults at the next iteration's generation check instead of reading a stale pointer. This is a widening only surface change, a `for` that rejected before now lowers, and the compiler source never spells the new form, so the previous release builds this source unchanged.

The container loop.

- `for x in v` over a `*Vector<T>` binds each element in index order. The iterand must be a pointer, so the loop can re-read the container's length and each element every iteration through the fat pointer; iterating a container by value is rejected, `iterate the container through a pointer, as in v: *Vector<T>`. The loop is not a builtin and code generation is untouched: the surface type pass records the classification in a side table and a new pass, `foriter`, rewrites each classified loop before mono into `__fit := v; mut __fi := 0; while __fi < vec_len(__fit) { x := vec_get(__fit, __fi); __fi = __fi + 1; if true { <body> } }`, all synthesized spans the for header. Mono instantiates `vec_len` and `vec_get` for the element type from the spliced calls on its ordinary worklist, and the ground double pass re-checks the rewritten types. The rewrite first appears in the mono dump; parse and desugar dumps stay byte-stable.
- `for k in m` over a `*Map<K, V>` binds each key, and `for k, v in m` binds each key with its value, both in the map's insertion order, its documented contract across overwrites, removes, and rehashing grows. The pair form is map only: two binders over any other iterand is rejected, `two loop names bind a map's key and value; this iterand has only elements`. Two new exported accessors back the pair form allocation free, `map_key_at(m, i)` and `map_val_at(m, i)`, reading the map's own insertion-order record rather than a copy.
- The index advances before the body, so a `continue`, which jumps to the while condition, has already moved past the current element; the loop terminates on a skipped iteration instead of spinning on the same element. The `if true` wrapper preserves the body's own scope, so a binder the body shadows nests legally, and the extra branch folds in `llvm`. Inside an async body the spliced locals live across an `await`: `__fit` and `__fi` become task frame slots reloaded on the resume edge, so the loop resumes with the container pointer and index intact.
- The container binder is a borrow the container still owns, the same as the result of `vec_get` or `map_key_at`. Mutating through a managed element is legal, but freeing or moving the binder is rejected, `cannot free a borrowed pointer; only its owner frees it` and `cannot move a borrowed pointer; only its owner can be moved`, because the accessors are non-owning views; the owning drain is `vec_take` or `map_take`. Ownership is judged on the intact `for` at the surface pass and the ground pass is types only, so a borrow cannot launder through the rewrite. The binder's element type is erased in the surface arena, so its width and type are recovered from the iterand's un-erased AST type when the iterand is a binding, which also lets a nested container loop over the binder classify; otherwise the ground pass recovers the width from the rewritten `vec_get` bind.
- The iterand is classified whether it is a plain name, a struct field, or a call result. A struct field read infers a surface wildcard, so the field's container type is recovered from the struct definition and the binding is annotated with it, which both keeps a field-held container from reaching codegen unrewritten and pins the accessor generics mono would otherwise fail to infer. A call iterand is typed from the callee's declared return type, so a nested loop over a call result needs no intermediate binding. The loader carries the pair form's second binder across a module merge, so an imported function that runs `for k, v in m` keeps its value binder instead of losing it to an undefined name.
- The surface pass owns container classification and the pre-mono rewrite consumes it; the types-only ground pass declines to classify, so a leftover container for a rewrite never reached is a loud cannot-iterate reject rather than a silent pass-through to codegen. The pair form over a map missing `map_val_at` is refused rather than under-binding to keys only, and two binders on an iterand whose type is not yet known stays permissive for the ground pass rather than falsely rejecting.
- The `for` binder is now typed, paying a probed silent-truncation and a link-death class. Over an array, slice, or string the binder carries its element type rather than a wildcard, so `y: int32 = x` over an `int64[N]` and a `string` bound to an integer element are check errors instead of a truncated runtime value or a `clang` death.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 907 records, 25 new since 1.10.1: the vector, pointer-element, generic, break-and-continue, in-body mutate, drain-take skip, async-await, nested, field-held, and call-result run goldens, the map key, pair, field-pair, grow-order, owned-keys, positional-accessor, and imported-pair goldens, the free-mid-loop generation fault, the borrowed-binder free and move twins, and the value-iterand, pair-over-vector, and binder width and type twins.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.10.1` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (d83a0535078cf74d14834845adc8323accfeb79af807ed0ea33627b64f176308) and one compiler IR sha256 (3c9964608e6ef20fc25d97c48b6739b970bec4edadabdbad1ac8471223be9fbb), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 907/907 under the ladder's own suite runs. The comment differential holds at 868 byte-equal comparisons over 902 files with zero failures. The ratchet holds: the `v1.10.1` release binary builds the 1.11.0 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green. The IR harness holds: every corpus file both compilers accept lowers to byte-identical LLVM IR, since a program that iterates no container is untouched by the rewrite.

## 1.10.1

The match tail. dusk rides the scope chain 1.10.0 landed to finish the match expression: an arm body that ends in a bare `match` now value-izes into the enclosing match, so a nested match is the arm's value with no name to bind it to, the teaching reject 1.9.0 shipped in place of the feature retires, and three adjacent judgment holes the feature exposed close with it. No new tokens and no new grammar, a form the checker rejected before now lowers, and the two soundness holes the fix closes each shipped a program that checked clean and then corrupted or read garbage.

The match tail.

- An arm body may end in a bare `match`. The last statement of a braced arm body, when it is a `match`, becomes the arm's yielded value: the inner match's arms supply what the outer arm hands up, and no name is bound to carry it. Before this release such a tail was a statement that stored nothing into the arm's result slot, so 1.9.0 rejected it with a message teaching the writer to bind the inner match to a name and yield the name; that reject is deleted and the form lowers directly. The rewrite happens once, before name resolution: the trailing match statement becomes an expression tail, so the type agreement, the ownership verdict, monomorphization, and code generation that already handle a nested match as an arm's value are reused wholesale, and a match written in statement position, an outer `match` that is not an arm's value, is untouched. The form nests to any depth, and both the type agreement and the ownership judgment penetrate every level.
- A silent double free through an inferred bind is sealed. An `x := match ...` with no annotation carries only a surface wildcard where ownership runs, so the checker could not see the result was a managed pointer and a borrowed result laundered into `x` unrecorded; a later `free(x)` then freed a pointer the subject still owned, a double free the generation backstop would fault at runtime rather than the check catching it. The inferred bind now consults the ownership verdict the match's arms agreed on and records it under the binding exactly as an annotated managed bind already did, so a match over a borrowed subject bound without an annotation refuses a later free, `cannot free a borrowed pointer; only its owner frees it`. This is the channel where the launder actually reached a free: a nested-arm borrow was intercepted by the mixed-arm reject in every case, and the inferred binding, not the nesting, is where a borrow crossed into a `free`.
- An arm that yields no value is rejected instead of reading garbage. An arm whose block ends in a statement that stores nothing, an `if` with no `else` or a bare `while`, left the single result slot uninitialized, and the outer bind read whatever the slot happened to hold, `5` then `0` by alloca luck in the probe. Every arm must now yield a value or diverge: an arm that neither yields a tail value nor `return`s, `break`s, or `continue`s out of the match is `every arm of a match expression must yield a value or return`, raise-only over the silent-garbage class.
- An all-nested match builds where it once died in `clang`. A match every one of whose arms is itself a match, the existing no-brace `A => match e2 { ... }` spelling, agreed on its type at check and then fell to the word-sized result slot fallback in code generation, so a program with a managed-pointer result checked clean and could not build. The result-slot typing now recurses into a nested-match arm, sizing the slot from the type the arms agreed on, so the accepted program builds and runs; this repair exists before the new block-tail surface and is exercised by it.
- The ownership verdict penetrates a nested match. The outer arm's verdict for a nested-match tail now consults the inner match's recorded verdict rather than judging the tail an owner unconditionally, so a borrow that reaches the arm through a nested match propagates outward to the free, move, return, and destructure positions rather than laundering through the nesting.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 882 records, 16 new since 1.10.0: the block-tail forms across plain, two-deep, owner-move, and binder-shadow shapes with the type, ownership, and borrow-free disagree twins, the every-arm-yields reject and its diverging-if accept, the float result slot pinned at its exact value, the inferred-binding and destructure launder rejects with the nested and generic variants, and the all-nested pointer repair.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.10.0` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (9e4fc4473af0d613e015ffb00b950fb56f16153ee2e9b1edb8be6ae4851f02a4) and one compiler IR sha256 (fd0cb02abe99ebb8415a287acc6453c02526039a7ca82ca5b292694660544ce0), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 882/882 under the ladder's own suite runs. The comment differential holds at 832 byte-equal comparisons over 876 files with zero failures. The ratchet holds: the `v1.10.0` release binary builds the 1.10.1 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.
- The ratchet holds: the `v1.10.0` release binary builds the 1.10.1 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. 1.10.1 widens acceptance only, a form the previous release rejected now lowers, and the compiler source never spells the new form, so the previous release builds this source unchanged; the tree may adopt block-tail match from 1.11.x on. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.

## 1.10.0

The scoped frame. dusk makes block scoping and shadowing sound the whole way down: the code generator gains a per-block scope chain in place of its flat per-function symbol table, so a binding that shadows an outer name in a nested block stops clobbering the outer name's resolution, the defer replay and the lambda capture that resolved through the same flat table are repaired with it, and the ambient allocator's name is reserved against a shadow that would hijack `alloc` and `free`. The type checker already resolved these correctly, so the shadowing and defer repairs change no check verdict; every one is a program that checked clean and then printed a wrong value, misdispatched a call, or died in `clang`, and each ships a run golden pinning the corrected output. The one new rejection reserves the allocator name, raise-only over a program that would otherwise misallocate through a shadow.

The scoped frame.

- Nested blocks resolve names per block. Before this release the code generator kept one flat map from a source name to its slot for the whole function, and a binding in a nested block overwrote the outer name's entry with nothing to restore it, so the outer name resolved to the inner slot for the rest of the function. Each construct that opens a block, an `if` branch, a `while` or `for` body, and a `match` arm, now owns a scope that ends with the block, and name resolution walks from the innermost open block outward to the function frame. This repairs a family of miscompiles at once: a same-type shadow that silently read the inner value after the block, an outer `x: int64 = 1` shadowed by an inner `x: int64 = 2` printing `2` where the restored outer read should print `1`; a cross-type shadow that left a `string` slot in scope so a later `x + 1` lowered against a string operand and died in `clang`, a legal program that could not build; a `for` loop variable that clobbered an outer name of the same name so the read after the loop returned the last iteration instead of the outer value; a `match` arm's payload binder that clobbered an outer local so the read after the match returned the arm's payload; a lambda placed after a shadowing block that captured the block's stale slot instead of the restored outer binding; and a closure-typed sibling binding that outlived its block so a later bare call misdispatched as a closure call against the dead local rather than reaching the top level function of the same name. The scope chain is compile time symbol table state only and emits no IR, so every program whose name resolution is unchanged lowers to byte-identical IR and only the repaired classes move.
- A `defer` resolves in the function frame. Replay runs at a return and reads the deferred expression's names there; before this release it read them through the same flat map, so a return from inside a block that shadowed a deferred name replayed against the shadow. Replay now resolves in the function frame the `defer` was registered in, which the top level only registration rule guarantees is the environment the `defer` was written against. A `defer println(x)` registered while `x` is `7`, then shadowed by a `string x` inside an `if` that returns from within it, prints `inner` from the block and `7` from the defer. The rule composes with a lambda's own frame: a `defer` inside a lambda replays in the lambda frame the same way, printing the lambda frame's binding rather than a shadow live at the lambda's return.
- The lambda capture walk is scoped too. A capture is resolved by name against the bindings visible where the lambda is written, and the collector that decides which outer names a lambda captures kept its own flat bound set that a nested block's binding polluted. A lambda-internal `if` that bound its own `x` left that `x` in the bound set when a later `return x` named the outer `x`, so the walk decided the name was locally bound, captured nothing, and the lambda died at identifier lowering; worse, when the masked outer name also existed as a top level function the under-capture resolved the later call to the global and returned the wrong value silently. The bound set is now cloned when the walk descends into a nested block or a match arm, so a block-local binding is confined to its block and a later outer use captures the outer binding.
- A binding that shadows the ambient allocator's name is rejected. A function with a `using` allocator parameter lowers bare `alloc` and `free` through that parameter's name, and the scope chain would otherwise let a nested binding of the same name hijack them. The checker refuses any binding that shadows the parameter's name, in the body or in a nested block, `cannot shadow the allocator parameter '<name>'; alloc and free in this function resolve through it, so rename this binding`, and the code generator resolves the allocator against the parameter regardless of a live shadow. This seals two hijack classes the scope chain would otherwise have opened: a shadow that is not an allocator sent `alloc` through a non-allocator and crashed with a `SIGSEGV`, and a same-type shadow silently drew from the wrong arena so the outer arena's used counter never advanced and the function returned a stale count. The reject fires whatever the shadow's type, since the danger is the name. A lambda body carries no ambient allocator of its own, so a binding of the reserved name inside a lambda stays legal and an `alloc` written there draws from the default heap.
- A method takes a `using` allocator like a free function does. Before this release the method lowering did not scan its parameters for `using`, so the ambient allocator stayed unset and bare `alloc` in a method body fell to the default heap, leaving the passed arena's used counter at `0`. A method with a `using` parameter now resolves `alloc` and `free` through it, so one `alloc(8)` in the body advances the arena and `used` prints `8`.
- A `using` parameter on an `async func` or a lambda is rejected. An `async func` re-materializes its parameters into a heap task frame a poll function reads across suspensions, and dispatching an ambient allocator through that frame is not wired, so the allocator was silently dropped and `alloc` fell to the default heap; the checker now refuses it, `an async func cannot take a using parameter yet; allocate before the call or use the default heap`, a loud reject until the feature lands. A lambda lowers to its own function with no ambient allocator, so a `using` parameter on a lambda was accepted and then dropped the same way; it is now `a lambda cannot take a using parameter; a lambda body has no ambient allocator, so allocate in the enclosing function or use the default heap`.
- A `defer` whose deferred expression opens its own scope resolves it correctly. A deferred expression that contains a `match` binds its arm payload names in that expression's own scopes, and those resolve normally; only a name that reaches outward to the enclosing function resolves in the registration frame rather than a shadow live at the return. The corner an earlier draft named as a defer and nested `match` binder residual is closed here, not carried.

The notes.

- One pre-existing limitation is unchanged and named for context. A lambda literal cannot be called directly at its definition site, the immediately invoked form, so an ambient allocator or a scoped binding cannot be threaded through such a call; the writer binds the lambda to a name and calls the name, or allocates in the enclosing function.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 866 records, 18 new since 1.9.1: the same-type and cross-type `if` shadows, the `for` variable shadow, the `match` arm binder shadow, the lambda capture after a shadow, the sibling closure-shadow dispatch, and the shadow-in-`if` across an `await`, the defer frame resolution and its lambda-frame twin, the lambda-internal sibling shadow and the masked call to a top level function, the allocator shadow rejects in the differing and same-type forms, the method `using` allocator advancing the arena, and the `async func` and lambda `using` parameter rejects; the shadowing pins re-verified byte-green, `scope_shadow_ok`, `matchexpr_armshadow_ok`, `samename_locals`, and the defer and await suite.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.9.1` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (727572782bb1f72651eaca6696001eede6810bb46fcefcfdc56341ca24becec7) and one compiler IR sha256 (d351acf89dcb8de31360c5e6204bdacd0627911f7ca384d8ada353e0026aa589), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 866/866 under the ladder's own suite runs. The comment differential holds at 818 byte-equal comparisons over 860 files with zero failures. The ratchet holds: the `v1.9.1` release binary builds the 1.10.0 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.
- The ratchet holds: the `v1.9.1` release binary builds the 1.10.0 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. The compiler source cannot depend on the repairs, since it compiles correctly today, which is what lets the previous release build this source unchanged. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.

## 1.9.1

The adoption. dusk closes the owning func ratchet and gains one vector helper: the standard library's `vec_take` and `map_take` now carry the `owning` marker themselves, the name and shape table that recognized them by name is deleted, and `std.vector` gains `vec_fold`. No new surface and no runtime change; the blessing a taken value carries is now the one declared mechanism every owning func uses, reproducing the name table's verdict on every existing take exactly.

The adoption.

- The standard takes carry the marker. `vec_take` and `map_take` are declared `owning func`, so they bless through the declared marker 1.9.0 shipped, and the checker's name and shape table, the by name recognition that blessed them since 1.8.0, is deleted along with its shape guard. The blessing on every existing take is the verdict the table gave, byte for byte, the full take suite green untouched, and the marker rides the import path into user code exactly as the table did. The one behavioral change is the spoof class: a user's own function named `vec_take` or `map_take` that is not marked `owning` is no longer blessed by its name, so a direct free of a container read through such a call is now rejected like any other unmarked func's, `cannot free a call result that may alias its arguments; vec_take or map_take removes an element as its owner`. A program that leaned on the old by name blessing marks the func `owning` to restore it; the flip is raise-only, named ahead of it in the 1.9.0 residuals and landed here.
- `std.vector` gains `vec_fold(v, init, f)`. It reduces a vector to a single value, folding `f` left to right over the elements from `init`, the accumulator its first argument and the current element its second, each call's result the next accumulator and the final one returned. An empty vector returns `init` with `f` uncalled, the source is neither mutated nor freed, and the accumulator type is independent of the element type, so a vector of one type folds into a value of another, a running sum, a joined string, or a built structure. It closes the 1.8.1 open note beside `vec_map` and `vec_filter`.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 848 records, 4 new since 1.9.0: the unmarked same-name take now rejecting a direct free with its legal twin proving the standard takes still bless through the import path, and the two vec_fold records covering the accumulator type independent of the element type and a capturing combiner.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.9.0` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (4d2e6b30afbfe124391737d847f876c838ed64bdca86e2cd20457515257bd301) and one compiler IR sha256 (c04596568cfacfb503c5b29c7ee53315da985791d06b157ce389c2d0b5638631), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 848/848 under the ladder's own suite runs. The comment differential holds at 790 byte-equal comparisons over 842 files with zero failures. The ratchet holds: the `v1.9.0` release binary builds the 1.9.1 source, marker-bearing stdlib included, and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.
- The ratchet holds: the `v1.9.0` release binary builds the 1.9.1 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. The standard library now declares owning funcs, and the previous release builds them because 1.9.0 shipped the marker path unioned with the name table it now deletes. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.

## 1.9.0

The owning func and the debt sweep. dusk gains the declared ownership marker and pays the 1.8.0 debt list in the same release: an `owning func` head declares its call result the caller's own value and the checker blesses it exactly as it blesses `vec_take`, a `match` used as an expression now types its result and judges its arms with one voice, and a batch of checker gaps that reached `clang` or the generation backstop close. No runtime value representation changes; the marker is checker only, and the one class of emitted IR that moves is the match expression result the fix deliberately re-types.

The owning func and the debt sweep.

- `owning func` is the declared generalization of the take blessing. A top level func head marked `owning` declares that its call result is the caller's own value, and the checker blesses every call to it the way it blesses `vec_take` and `map_take`, exempting the result from the container alias condemnation, so the caller may `free` or `move` it. `owning` is a context sensitive modifier positioned like `async`, `owning func grab(...) -> *T` and `export owning func ...`, mutually exclusive with `async` and rejected as `export "C" owning`. It is rejected on a method, `a method cannot be owning; owning marks a top level func`, on `main`, `main cannot be owning; nothing calls main to take its result`, which also pins the newline attach trap where a stray `owning` on its own line would bind to the next func head, on a `void` return, `an owning func returns the one value it hands over; it cannot return void`, on a tuple return, `an owning func returns the one value it hands over; it returns one value, not a tuple`, and on a `*raw T` return, `an owning func returns a managed value; a raw pointer carries no generation to check`, each a named error at the head or the declaration. A `foreign` return never admits the word. A bare generic return is legal, the `vec_take` shape, inert at a non pointer instantiation where no result is an owner. The body owes nothing statically: `owning` is a declared contract, and a lying marker is carried by the generation backstop to a named `fatal: use of a freed or stale pointer at path:line` at the misuse site rather than corrupting silently. The marker ships unioned with the standard library's name and shape blessing of the two takes, which carries a user `owning func` across the import path this release; the standard takes adopt the marker and the name table retires in 1.9.1.
- A `match` used as an expression is corrected and extended. Its result now types from the value its arms yield, laid out as the fat pointer every carrier uses, so `x: *int64 = match *t { Leaf(q) => move(q), Empty => alloc(0) }` builds where both the move and the copy spelling died in `clang` before, the result slot having been typed `i64` while the arms stored a fat pointer. The arms must agree on that type: an arm whose value disagrees in width or kind, an `int32` beside an `int64` or a pointer beside an integer, is rejected at check, `the arms of a match expression must agree on type`, which retires a silent truncation where the result slot took the first arm's narrower type and a wider arm's value came back mangled, `5000000000` through an `int32` slot returning `705032704`. That agreed type is now the match's own type, so a match bound or passed against an annotation is width and type checked like any other value, and a match used as another match's arm value carries its type into the outer agreement rather than passing as an unchecked wildcard. A bare `match` written as an arm's value is rejected, `a match arm cannot yield a bare match; bind the inner match to a name and yield that name`, since the parser lowers it to a statement that stores nothing into the arm's slot and the outer match would return an uninitialized result. On the corrected result the checker judges ownership with one voice, judged with each arm scope live so an arm local fresh owner reads as the move out it is rather than a borrow: the arms must agree, an owner only when every value yielding arm yields an owner, a `move` of a payload binder, an `alloc`, an arm local owner, or an owning call among them, and a borrow only when every arm borrows; a bare owner binder arm is rejected as the copy of an owner, and a mix is rejected at the odd arm, `the arms of a match expression must agree on ownership of its result`. A borrowed subject yields a borrow through every spelling, so extracting its payload and freeing the result is a borrowed free, and returning that result is refused the same way, `cannot return a borrowed match result; move the payload or return an owning call`, closing the return, tuple destructure, and `free`/`move` argument positions that once laundered the borrow into the caller's owner.
- The checker closes four gaps that passed check and failed later. A ground `free` of a value that is not a heap pointer, an `int64` reached through a generic instantiation among them, is now `free takes a heap pointer, not <ty>` at check rather than an IR failure in `clang`, `move` exempt, and the same message now catches a `free` of a scalar struct field, `free(n.count)` with an `int64` `count`, the one operand shape that reached the heap check untyped. A `match` arm that yields a lambda literal is `a match arm cannot yield a lambda; bind the function to a name outside the match`. A constructor or pattern naming a variant the enum does not declare is `'<Enum>' has no variant '<name>'` at both the qualified `Shape.Oval(1.0)` and the bare pattern `Oval(r)`, where the missing variant bound silently and died in codegen before. An argument of the wrong type spelled as a struct field access, `str_eq(n.name, "x")` with an `int64` `name` field, now reaches the argument check and is `argument N has the wrong type`, the one shape that had slipped past untyped.
- The dead duplicate statement parser is removed. `parsestmt.dusk` was never imported and exported the same three names `parseexpr.dusk` exports, so the loader could never have merged it without the duplicate definition check rejecting the build; the file was dead weight kept in sync by hand. Its deletion changes no diagnostic, dump, or emitted IR on any input; the compiler binary changes and the stage ladder re-fixes as at any release.
- `dusk doc` binds a doc comment to a `foreign` function head. A `/** */` block before a declaration inside a `foreign` block now attaches to that function, `dusk doc` renders a Foreign Functions section, and `dusk doc --json` emits each foreign entry under its own kind with the head's byte span, additive keys a consumer that does not know them ignores. A dangling doc inside a `foreign` block is reported like any other.

The residuals, named not hidden.

- A user `owning func` named after a builtin, `move` for one, is accepted and contributes a blessing but is unreachable, the builtin winning dispatch, the pre-existing reserved name class. The name and shape blessing of a user `vec_take` or `map_take` of the matching shape still stands beside the marker this release, and the unannotated bind of a container read, the opaque callee read, and the conservative fresh box wrapper reject carry forward from 1.8.0 unchanged. When 1.9.1 deletes the name table, a user program that leaned on the by name blessing of its own `vec_take` loses it and must mark the func `owning`; the flip is raise-only and named here ahead of it. Every one of these is a leak or a named runtime fault, never a silent corruption.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 844 records, 42 new since 1.8.1: the owning surface with its parse, return-shape, method, main, and raw twins and the lying-marker and double-free fault pins, the match expression matrix across result typing, arm agreement on type and on ownership, the arm-local, binder-alias, and shadow shapes, the return, destructure, and argument position rejects, and the nested and annotation agreement pins, the unknown-variant, field-argument, ground-free, and arm-lambda closes with their legal twins, the tuple-element take pin, and the foreign doc markdown, json, drift, and dangling records.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.8.1` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (800324924cdae93b96b0144723bcaa729f7fe6a9eb25ab0462ddad676ae03f72) and one compiler IR sha256 (ebeda1fe9b05aaf51441f0270b41d03c65842f6eeefd5eeac2e14205f9ce7a0e), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 844/844 under the ladder's own suite runs. The comment differential holds at 784 byte-equal comparisons over 838 files with zero failures.
- The ratchet holds: the `v1.8.1` release binary builds the 1.9.0 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. The compiler, standard library, and tools declare no owning func of their own, which is why the previous release builds this source unchanged; only examples use the marker, and the standard library adopts it next release. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.

## 1.8.1

The stdlib five. dusk gains five standard library items and no new surface: `std.flags` for command line parsing, `std.set` over the generic map, `vec_map` and `vec_filter` beside the vector sort already there, `std.time`'s `weekday` and a strict `parse_iso8601`, and `std.json`'s `json_free`, the first in-tree caller of the owning takes 1.8.0 introduced. Every item is plain dusk in `lib/std/` with its examples and goldens; no token, no AST node, no checker rule, no lowering, and no runtime change. The release rests on 1.8.0: `json_free` is the hand written deep free of a parse tree, and the checker blessing that lets its `vec_take` and `map_take` calls own their results is exactly what 1.8.0 shipped.

The stdlib five.

- `std.flags` parses a command line as a register then parse builder in `lib/std/flags.dusk`. A program registers each flag up front with `flag_bool`, `flag_str`, or `flag_int`, then hands the whole `argv` to `flags_parse(f, argv, start)`, which fills the values and collects the positional words in order; there are no short flags and no grouping, a flag is its long name matched as `--name`. A lone `--` ends flag parsing, `--name=value` and `--name value` both bind, a bool flag takes no value, and any token that does not begin with `--`, a single dash word or a negative number included, is positional and keeps its order, a repeated flag last wins. Bad input is an error value the caller must handle, `unknown flag '--frob'`, `flag '--timeout' needs a value`, `flag '--timeout' needs an integer, got 'abc'`, or `flag '--verbose' takes no value`, so the library never prints and never exits on a user's typo; a misuse of the library aborts with a `fatal: flags:` prefix, a duplicate registration, a getter on the wrong kind, or a positional index out of range. `flags_usage` returns a deterministic caller owned string, and `Flags` stores only borrows, so `flags_free` releases its two vectors alone.
- `std.set` is `Set<T>`, an unordered set over `std.map` keyed by the element with an always true `bool`, in `lib/std/set.dusk`. The wrap is a real type, so a `*Set<string>` parameter cannot take a map, and membership is exactly key presence with no present but false state to hold. `set_add` and `set_remove` report whether the call changed the set, `set_items` returns the members in first insertion order as a fresh caller owned vector, and the element contract is the map key contract verbatim, a hashable type compared with `==` whose string elements are borrowed and outlive the set. `set_free` releases the backing map and nothing else.
- `std.vector` gains `vec_map(v, f)` and `vec_filter(v, p)`, each returning a fresh caller owned vector and leaving the source untouched, the closure called exactly once per element in index order. `vec_map` infers its result type from the closure; `vec_filter` copies through the elements the predicate keeps. Both take a capturing lambda or a named function like `vec_sort`, and neither is paradigm gated. `vec_sort` already takes its comparator as a closure, so there is no separate `vec_sort_by`. `vec_filter`'s copy means a managed pointer element is aliased by both vectors, a double free the checker does not catch across the call and the generational backstop faults instead.
- `std.time` gains `weekday(c)`, the `tm_wday` day of week with 0 for Sunday through 6 for Saturday, `1970-01-01` returning 4 and pre epoch dates correct through the flooring modulo; and `parse_iso8601(s) -> (Civil, error)`, the strict inverse of `format_iso8601`. The parser reads exactly `[-]Y{4,}-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` with uppercase `T` and `Z`, no fractions, no offset, and no lowercase, and validates as an input boundary, the day checked by round tripping through the civil day count so a leap day is exact and a leap second `:60` rejected, returning `iso8601: malformed timestamp` on a structural mismatch and `iso8601: field out of range` on a bad field. The roundtrip law `parse_iso8601(format_iso8601(c)) == c` holds for every representable `Civil`, negative and five digit years included.
- `std.json` gains `json_free(root)`, the hand written worklist deep free of a parse tree moved verbatim from the 1.8.0 acceptance example, the first in-tree caller of `vec_take` and `map_take`. It consumes `root`, freeing every node, string payload, object key, and backing buffer, so no pointer into the tree is valid afterward and a later dereference faults named through the generation check. It requires a fully heap allocated tree, which every `json_parse` result is, and a hand built tree carrying a literal string payload frees that payload into undefined behavior; a subtree reachable from two parents double frees with the second free faulting named. The example's own local `json_free` is renamed first so the standard export does not collide in the merged module.

The notes.

- `std.set` rejects a pointer element, `Set<*Json>` for one, through the backing map's `hash` and `==` restriction, and that diagnostic locates inside `map.dusk`, the pre-existing `Map<*T, V>` class rather than anything set adds. `vec_filter`'s aliasing of managed pointer elements is documented, not checked, and backstopped by the generational free check like every other take-class misuse. `json_free` is the first standard library code to call the owning takes, so this library cannot be backported onto a compiler older than 1.8.0, whose checker would reject the blessed `free` of a taken value; the standard export keeps its name through the privatize pass, which is what carries the blessing across the import path. `json_parse` now reclaims a repeated object key's shadowed value: `{"a":1,"a":2}` keeps the later value as it always did, and the earlier subtree plus the repeat key's bytes are freed at parse time instead of leaking beyond every later `json_free`.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 802 records, 20 new since 1.8.0: the flags grammar, errors, faults, and byte-pinned usage text with the must-handle twin, the set semantics and dedup with the pointer-element reject twin, the four vec_map and vec_filter shapes including the narrowing and struct instantiations, the time round-trip law over epoch, leap day, negative and five digit years, the weekday anchors, the parse error sweep and the negative zero reject, and the three json_free records, the parse-emit-free roundtrip proving reclamation by its post-free fault, the hand built all-heap tree freed clean, and the duplicate key document parsed, emitted, and freed with the shadowed subtree reclaimed.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.8.0` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (0a78f38dfd4178b9a7b4856192a9b52f3d1370cb488f7bdf8ea45ca7b1e3b95e) and one compiler IR sha256 (75feb78dd51e5058a7e433f57f0afa294e8908c83b5e6a7b5a2850ecebc74e3c), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 802/802 under the ladder's own suite runs. The comment differential holds at 760 byte-equal comparisons over 798 files with zero failures.
- The ratchet holds: the `v1.8.0` release binary builds the 1.8.1 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.

## 1.8.0

The owning take. dusk gains owning removal from a container: `vec_take` and `map_take` take an element out and hand the caller the owner, the checker's borrow net around containers settles into one model across every spelling, and a program can now hand write the deep free of a heap tree, the json tree the acceptance. No parser, codegen, or runtime change: the removal is two standard library generics plus a checker that blesses their results as owners.

The owning take.

- `vec_take(v, i)` removes the element at index `i`, shifts every survivor after it down one slot so the order is preserved, shortens the vector, and returns the removed element as an owned value, so a taken managed pointer is the caller's to `free`. Taking the last index shifts nothing, so `while vec_len(v) > 0 { vec_take(v, vec_len(v) - 1) }` drains from the back in `O(1)` per step, the idiom the spec teaches since a forward walk over a vector being taken from skips the successor that shifted into the vacated slot. It bounds checks exactly as `vec_get`, a negative index or one at or past the length aborting `fatal: vector index out of bounds`.
- `map_take(m, k)` probes for `k`, removes its entry with the same slot clear, cluster repair, and insertion-order forget `map_remove` does, and returns the stored value as an owned value. A missing key aborts `fatal: map_take of a missing key`, so probe with `map_has` first. It frees nothing, ever, not the value's block and not the key's heap bytes, which belong to whoever else holds the string, so removal and reclamation stay separate acts.
- The checker's borrow net around containers is one model across every spelling now. Freeing a container read directly, `free(vec_get(v, 0))`, is rejected with `cannot free a call result that may alias its arguments; vec_take or map_take removes an element as its owner`, the `move` form with the matching message, and the method spelling `free(box.get(0))` rejects the same way. A match payload binder tracks its subject: under a deref of an owned pointer every managed binder is an owner and frees, under a borrowed subject every binder borrows and refuses, so the raw binder and its annotated rebind agree with one voice where they diverged by spelling before.
- A match payload binder is typed from the subject's enum, so `vec_len(items)` on a `*Vector<*Json>` payload infers its type parameter with no rebinding workaround, and an owner binder correctly demands `move` or direct use rather than a silent copy. A variant pattern that binds the wrong number of payload fields is a check error, `variant pattern '<V>' binds <got> of <want> payload field(s)`, at both match sites.
- `examples/jsonfree.dusk` is the acceptance: a worklist deep free that parses a json document, emits it, and reclaims every node, string payload, object key, and backing buffer, taking each node out of its container as an owner. A guarded probe reads the freed root afterward and faults named, `fatal: use of a freed or stale pointer at examples/jsonfree.dusk:71`, which is the proof the blocks actually retired. The recursive spelling stays inexpressible by design, since a parameter borrows, and the spec teaches the worklist as the one idiom.

The residuals, named not hidden.

- An unannotated bind of a container read, `q := vec_get(v, 0); free(q)`, passes the check and faults at runtime, since a bare generic return is not substituted on the pass where ownership runs and the read is never classified as managed; a container read through an opaque callee, a lambda value or a dynamically dispatched interface method, passes the same way; a user's own exported `vec_take` or `map_take` of the matching shape is blessed by name and shape, narrowed by the shape guard and left to the runtime backstop; freeing a fresh-box wrapper's result directly is rejected conservatively where a named bind is accepted; and extracting an owned payload through a `match` expression is limited, the take idiom the sanctioned path. Every one of these is a leak or a named runtime fault, never a silent corruption.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 782 records, 32 new since 1.7.1: the vec_take and map_take data semantics with their bounds and missing-key faults, the direct, method, and bound container-read rejects with their legal twins, the typed-binder inference, the owned and borrowed match binder pair, the shared-node and match-twice double free faults, the borrow launder fault, the documented hole B and opaque residual pins, and the `examples/jsonfree.dusk` roundtrip and post-free fault.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.7.1` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (551222cef0b8f195d3c3cbf5de2548634629f909b336ae4e7cb4d9075744419e) and one compiler IR sha256 (5a62cf47ea2949cab51bf52f8bdbd161bca6c70fc462f85cf9d62f42ab248a82), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 782/782 under the ladder's own suite runs. The comment differential holds at 724 byte-equal comparisons over 778 files with zero failures.
- The ratchet holds: the `v1.7.1` release binary builds the 1.8.0 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. The compiler, standard library, and tools call neither new take and rely on no typed-binder inference, which is why the previous release builds this source unchanged; `vec_take` and `map_take` ship as definitions, goldens, and the one example here, and the first in-tree caller lands next. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green.

## 1.7.1

The machine face. The compiler's diagnostics and its doc model are now data a tool consumes, not text a person scrapes: `dusk check --json` emits one deterministic JSON document with every diagnostic's message and source span, and the doc model gains a span key on every item, the two contracts an editor integration builds on. No language surface changes.

The machine face.

- `dusk check --json <file>` runs the exact pipeline `check` runs and reports the result as data. stdout is exactly one JSON document plus one trailing newline in every outcome, clean or broken, stderr stays silent, and the exit code mirrors the human command, a read error on the root included, reported inside the envelope rather than beside it. Diagnostics appear in the order the human command prints them, so the two faces never disagree about what went wrong first.
- A span carries file local byte offsets, `lo` and `hi` clamped to UTF-8 boundaries, plus 1 based line and column pairs counted in Unicode scalars at both ends. Bytes are the primitive a tool converts from, the precomputed pairs serve a consumer that wants them, and a multibyte line is pinned by a golden where the scalar column and the byte extent visibly diverge. A diagnostic in an imported file names that file and carries offsets local to it, and a diagnostic with no position, an unresolvable import for one, carries a null span.
- Underneath, the loader's errors stopped being pre-rendered strings: each is now a structured record carrying its path, span, and message, and the human renderer consumes the same records the JSON face does, so the two can never drift. The human output is byte identical to 1.7.0, which every existing failing-check golden proves by not changing.
- The doc model gains positions: every item object carries a `span` key with its name token's location, the impl object its `impl` keyword's, and every doc object opens with the span of its whole doc block, delimiters included. The consumers-ignore-unknown-keys rule is now written into the spec for both machine surfaces, which is what makes an additive key a non-event from here on.
- `severity` is always `"error"` today and the key exists so a warning can arrive without a schema break; a `stage` key was deliberately left out, since freezing pipeline internals into a stable contract serves no consumer.

Numbers.

- `testrun tests/goldens.manifest` passes all 750 records, 9 new since 1.7.0: the clean, type error, parse error, imported-file span, null span, read error, UTF-8 divergence, and human parity records for `check --json`, and the span-bearing doc model golden, with four existing doc model goldens regenerated for the added keys.
- The stage ladder re-fixed with the `v1.7.0` release binary as seed: stage1, stage2, and stage3 share one binary sha256 (4fc3de88...) and one compiler IR sha256 (ce46c3ba...), the collapse, fixpoint, and determinism checks all green, 750/750 under the ladder's own suite runs.
- The ratchet holds: the `v1.7.0` release binary builds the 1.7.1 source and the freshly built compiler passes the full suite. dawn stays byte compatible, 10 of 10 offline checks green, and the comment differential still holds, 682 rewritten variants byte equal.

## 1.7.0

The debt release. Every entry in the known defect ledger was re-verified against the current compiler instead of trusted from memory, and the verdicts drove the release: three entries were already closed by earlier work and are now pinned by goldens and spec text, one had shrunk from a codegen bug to a diagnostics problem, one turned out to be inexpressible in the ownership model and is now documented honestly, and one was a live soundness hole, a program the checker accepted that read a dead stack frame at runtime. That hole is closed.

The escape hole.

- A function that wrapped a pointer argument into a returned struct lost the connection on both sides of the escape analysis: the summary walk joined an aggregate member only when its declared field type was a view, so a buried pointer dropped out, and the alias linker had no arm for a plain call, so the result binding never joined the argument's group. A frame view stored through the wrapper then egressed unseen, and the built program read a dead frame. `examples/escalias_wrap_residual.dusk`, tracked as a residual since the escape work landed, built and ran on this hole; it now rejects at the `return`.
- The fix is raise only, on both sides. The summary walk now classifies each aggregate member three ways: a view member joins whole as before, a member that reaches a managed pointer contributes its origins and reads with the frame flag cleared, so a pointer member never frame-taints the literal it sits in, and a scalar member still contributes nothing. An array literal of pointers keeps its elements' reads the same way. The alias linker gains the missing call arm: a call whose result type is or reaches a managed pointer links the result binding to every argument the callee's summary names as returned or read through, filtered by the same per-name rules every other alias source obeys, and a projection straight off a call, `wrap(c).c`, roots at the call the same way. Opaque callees link nothing at the binding, deliberately; the store side already treats an unknown callee as reaching every argument, so the conservative net was and remains closed there.
- The full suite passed unchanged apart from the residual flipping to a reject, and the compiler, standard library, and tools all build under the tightened check. One new coarseness is accepted and named: linking is by binding, not by field, so storing a frame view through one field of a call's returned aggregate raises the whole group even when the view lands in a heap backed member; the direct spelling through the member's own binding stays accepted.

The rest of the ledger.

- A type declaration inside a function body was a parse cascade, one bewildered error per token to the end of the file. It is now a single error, `an enum is declared at the top level, not inside a function body`, with the keyword substituted, and the parser skips the misplaced block and keeps checking the rest of the body. One shared recovery routine serves both statement parsers.
- The interface-as-generic-argument rejection, in the compiler since the escape era, is now in the spec with a golden pinning `Box<Speaker>`; the reasoning is recorded where generics are defined, an interface names a set of types and monomorphization needs one.
